### Live-E2T: Real-time Threat Monitoring in Video via Deduplicated Event Reasoning and Chain-of-Thought
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18571v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhan Wang, Cheng Liu, Zihan Zhao, Weichao Wu
- **Abstract**: Real-time threat monitoring identifies threatening behaviors in video streams and provides reasoning and assessment of threat events through explanatory text. However, prevailing methodologies, whether based on supervised learning or generative models, struggle to concurrently satisfy the demanding requirements of real-time performance and decision explainability. To bridge this gap, we introduce Live-E2T, a novel framework that unifies these two objectives through three synergistic mechanisms. First, we deconstruct video frames into structured Human-Object-Interaction-Place semantic tuples. This approach creates a compact, semantically focused representation, circumventing the information degradation common in conventional feature compression. Second, an efficient online event deduplication and updating mechanism is proposed to filter spatio-temporal redundancies, ensuring the system's real time responsiveness. Finally, we fine-tune a Large Language Model using a Chain-of-Thought strategy, endow it with the capability for transparent and logical reasoning over event sequences to produce coherent threat assessment reports. Extensive experiments on benchmark datasets, including XD-Violence and UCF-Crime, demonstrate that Live-E2T significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods in terms of threat detection accuracy, real-time efficiency, and the crucial dimension of explainability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Live-E2T, a novel framework for real-time, interpretable threat monitoring in video streams. It addresses the challenges of balancing real-time performance and decision explainability, which are often lacking in existing supervised learning or generative model-based approaches. Live-E2T deconstructs video frames into "Human-Object-Interaction-Place" (HOIP) semantic tuples, employs an efficient event deduplication and updating mechanism, and utilizes a Chain-of-Thought (CoT) fine-tuned Large Language Model (LLM) to provide coherent threat assessment reports. The authors demonstrate through experiments on XD-Violence and UCF-Crime datasets that Live-E2T outperforms state-of-the-art methods in terms of threat detection accuracy, real-time efficiency, and explainability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its holistic approach to combining existing techniques into a unified framework that addresses the crucial trade-off between real-time performance and explainability. While each component (semantic tuple extraction, event deduplication, and CoT-LLM reasoning) may have precedents, the synergistic combination and optimization for real-time threat monitoring are noteworthy. Deconstructing complex scenes into structured semantic tags (HOIP) before generating descriptions is a novel choice, and could provide useful real-time benefits.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is substantial, as it targets a critical area in video surveillance – threat monitoring. The ability to provide interpretable threat assessments in real-time is highly valuable for security applications where trust and accountability are essential. If the results are truly reproducible and the system scalable, Live-E2T could have a significant impact on how security systems are designed and deployed. Furthermore, designing the system with these characteristics would be extremely useful and efficient.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a relevant problem:** Real-time, interpretable threat monitoring is a pressing need in video surveillance.
    *   **Holistic framework:** Live-E2T offers a well-integrated approach that combines different techniques effectively.
    *   **Experimental validation:** The paper includes comprehensive experiments on benchmark datasets, demonstrating superior performance compared to existing methods.
    *   **Emphasis on Explainability:** A key strength is the focus on interpretable outputs, which enhances trust and transparency.
    *   **Consideration of real-time constraints:** The explicit focus on real-time processing distinguishes it from many existing approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on LLMs:** The reliance on a large language model may introduce potential biases or inconsistencies in reasoning, potentially requiring careful monitoring. There is also the computation overhead of such complex tasks, which should be clearly discussed.
    *   **Limited discussion on scalability:** While the paper claims real-time performance, the scalability of the framework to large-scale deployments with numerous camera feeds is not thoroughly addressed.
    *   **Lack of detailed discussion on failure cases:** Although it does highlight some issues, more discussion about situations where the model struggles would enhance credibility.
    *   **Dependency on external resources**: the paper discusses fine-tuning datasets based on previous annotation schemes. Since these techniques have been applied already, it would be helpful to discuss the impact of varying these annotation methods.

*   **Potential Influence:** Live-E2T has the potential to influence the development of next-generation intelligent surveillance systems. Its modular design could inspire the development of more explainable and trustworthy AI systems for security applications. The system could become a benchmark for real-time performance and interpretability. The use of textual narratives to represent the events can also be a major advantage for quick identification of high-risk scenarios.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper introduces a novel and valuable framework with demonstrable improvements in real-time, interpretable threat monitoring. The weaknesses regarding reliance on LLMs, scalability, and specific failure modes are important considerations but do not diminish the overall contribution. The framework is timely and addresses a vital problem area. Although it builds upon existing components, the holistic integration and focus on real-time explainability warrant a positive evaluation.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV

### The Ranking Blind Spot: Decision Hijacking in LLM-based Text Ranking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18575v1)
- **Authors**: Yaoyao Qian, Yifan Zeng, Yuchao Jiang, Chelsi Jain, Huazheng Wang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong performance in information retrieval tasks like passage ranking. Our research examines how instruction-following capabilities in LLMs interact with multi-document comparison tasks, identifying what we term the "Ranking Blind Spot", a characteristic of LLM decision processes during comparative evaluation. We analyze how this ranking blind spot affects LLM evaluation systems through two approaches: Decision Objective Hijacking, which alters the evaluation goal in pairwise ranking systems, and Decision Criteria Hijacking, which modifies relevance standards across ranking schemes. These approaches demonstrate how content providers could potentially influence LLM-based ranking systems to affect document positioning. These attacks aim to force the LLM ranker to prefer a specific passage and rank it at the top. Malicious content providers can exploit this weakness, which helps them gain additional exposure by attacking the ranker. In our experiment, We empirically show that the proposed attacks are effective in various LLMs and can be generalized to multiple ranking schemes. We apply these attack to realistic examples to show their effectiveness. We also found stronger LLMs are more vulnerable to these attacks. Our code is available at: https://github.com/blindspotorg/RankingBlindSpot
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the concept of a "Ranking Blind Spot" in LLM-based text ranking systems. It argues that LLMs, when used for multi-document comparison tasks, become uniquely vulnerable to manipulation due to their instruction-following capabilities and difficulty in prioritizing task-specific knowledge over injected instructions.  The paper proposes a framework called "Decision Hijacking" that encompasses two attack strategies: Decision Objective Hijacking (DOH), which alters the evaluation goal entirely, and Decision Criteria Hijacking (DCH), which modifies the standards of relevance. The authors empirically demonstrate the effectiveness of these attacks on various LLM architectures and ranking schemes, finding that stronger models are paradoxically more vulnerable.  They also conduct realistic attack experiments using search engine results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper identifies a specific vulnerability unique to LLMs in ranking tasks, which is novel. Prior work has examined adversarial attacks on neural rankers and prompt injection, but this paper focuses on the specific interaction between LLM instruction-following and multi-document comparison, leading to a new type of vulnerability.  The framework of "Decision Hijacking" and its two sub-strategies (DOH and DCH) provide a useful lens for analyzing and categorizing such attacks.
*   **Significance:** The findings have important implications for the security and reliability of LLM-based ranking systems. As LLMs are increasingly used in search engines, recommendation systems, and other information retrieval tasks, understanding and mitigating these vulnerabilities is crucial. The discovery that more powerful models are more susceptible to these attacks is concerning and highlights the need for architectural solutions rather than simple patches.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition: The "Ranking Blind Spot" is a well-defined and compelling concept.
    *   Comprehensive framework: The Decision Hijacking framework provides a structured approach to analyzing the vulnerabilities.
    *   Empirical validation: The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed attacks across different LLMs and ranking schemes.
    *   Realistic experiments: The use of web pages from search engines increases the real-world relevance of the findings.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited attack vectors: While DOH and DCH cover a range of scenarios, other potential attack vectors may exist that were not explored. For example, what would a mixed approach look like and is there an optimal prompting strategy for launching attacks.
    *   Limited mitigation strategies: The paper suggests instructional separation, adversarial fine-tuning, and semantic anomaly detection, but does not explore these solutions in detail. More concrete mitigation strategies and their effectiveness would significantly strengthen the paper.
    *   Dataset dependence: Although TREC is a well-established benchmark, other datasets might reveal different vulnerability profiles. It would benefit from a more diverse dataset and attack set.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper is likely to stimulate further research on the security of LLM-based ranking systems. It provides a foundation for developing more robust evaluation metrics and mitigation techniques. The finding that stronger models are more vulnerable could shift research efforts towards developing more "defensively-oriented" LLM architectures.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper makes a significant and novel contribution by identifying and characterizing the "Ranking Blind Spot" vulnerability. The Decision Hijacking framework is a useful tool for understanding and analyzing these attacks. While the empirical validation is strong, the limited exploration of mitigation strategies and possible datasets are weaknesses. Overall, the paper is a valuable contribution to the field, but there is room for further research to explore mitigation strategies and additional attack vectors.

The contribution is not groundbreaking enough to warrant a higher score, as it mainly builds on existing prompt injection attacks, but the specific focus on ranking and the identification of the vulnerability in stronger models is a substantial contribution. Also, the weakness of not exploring mitigation strategies holds it back.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Prior-based Noisy Text Data Filtering: Fast and Strong Alternative For Perplexity
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18577v1)
- **Authors**: Yeongbin Seo, Gayoung Kim, Jaehyung Kim, Jinyoung Yeo
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) are pretrained on massive web corpora, careful selection of data becomes essential to ensure effective and efficient learning. While perplexity (PPL)-based filtering has shown strong performance, it suffers from drawbacks: substantial time costs and inherent unreliability of the model when handling noisy or out-of-distribution samples. In this work, we propose a simple yet powerful alternative: a prior-based data filtering method that estimates token priors using corpus-level term frequency statistics, inspired by linguistic insights on word roles and lexical density. Our approach filters documents based on the mean and standard deviation of token priors, serving as a fast proxy to PPL while requiring no model inference. Despite its simplicity, the prior-based filter achieves the highest average performance across 20 downstream benchmarks, while reducing time cost by over 1000x compared to PPL-based filtering. We further demonstrate its applicability to symbolic languages such as code and math, and its dynamic adaptability to multilingual corpora without supervision
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to noisy text data filtering for pretraining large language models (LLMs).  Instead of relying on perplexity (PPL)-based filtering, which is computationally expensive and can be unreliable on out-of-distribution data, the authors propose a prior-based filtering method.  This method estimates token priors using corpus-level term frequency statistics. Documents are then filtered based on the mean and standard deviation of these token priors.  The authors argue that this approach is inspired by linguistic insights relating word roles and lexical density, and it serves as a fast and effective proxy for PPL-based filtering without requiring model inference. Experiments on downstream tasks demonstrate that their method achieves higher average performance than PPL-based filtering while significantly reducing time cost. The method is also shown to be applicable to symbolic languages and multilingual corpora without supervision.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using term frequency statistics to estimate token priors and filter data is novel, especially in the context of LLM pretraining. While term frequency analysis has historical roots in linguistics and information retrieval, applying it as a lightweight alternative to perplexity for data filtering is a fresh perspective. The paper effectively connects this method to linguistic principles, providing a theoretical grounding for the approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a crucial problem in LLM training: efficient and effective data selection. PPL-based filtering, despite its effectiveness, suffers from high computational costs, making it difficult to scale to massive web datasets. The proposed method addresses this limitation by offering a much faster alternative. The demonstrated improvements in downstream task performance compared to PPL-based filtering is a significant result, showing that this simple technique can outperform more complex methods.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Efficiency:** The primary strength is the dramatic reduction in computational cost compared to PPL-based filtering (1000x speedup).
    *   **Effectiveness:** The method demonstrates superior or comparable performance on various downstream tasks.
    *   **Generalizability:** Application to symbolic languages and multilingual corpora enhances the method's broad applicability.
    *   **Theoretical Grounding:**  The connection to linguistic principles adds theoretical weight to the empirical results.
    *   **Ablation and Analysis:** The paper provides a reasonable set of ablation studies (effect of using mean vs stdev of priors, role of the tokenizer and block size), and analyzes data loss in specific scenarios, which strengthen the claims.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simplicity:** While the simplicity is a strength, it also raises questions about the method's limitations. There are cases where PPL can capture finer-grained semantics than simple term frequencies.
    *   **Reliance on Term Frequency:**  The method's effectiveness is predicated on the relationship between term frequency and word role, which might not hold equally well across all domains or text types. The paper acknowledges this and offers a few potential solutions for improving the prior (calculate the prior on blended data, using std instead of mean).
    *   **Lack of comparison against more competitive methods**: The study does not conduct comparisons with recent data curation strategies that include methods such as active learning, curriculum learning, reinforcement learning, and optimal transport for identifying and prioritizing valuable data.
    *   **Data-centric focus**: Data-centric approach lacks a comprehensive strategy for handling more intricate challenges, for instance, the memorization of private information. This implies potential vulnerabilities regarding privacy and data leakage in critical applications.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the field by shifting the focus towards simpler, more efficient data filtering techniques. It could also inspire further research into the relationship between linguistic statistics and LLM training. Given the growing size of training datasets, efficient filtering methods are crucial, and this work offers a promising direction.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a novel and efficient approach to a critical problem in LLM pretraining, supported by empirical evidence and theoretical grounding. While the simplicity of the method might limit its applicability in certain niche scenarios, the overall significance and potential influence on the field are substantial. The method does not include a comparison against more competitive methods such as active learning or curriculum learning. Despite this, the efficient and effective performance on language pre-training is a significant advancement, resulting in the following score:

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Photographer Eye: Teaching Multimodal Large Language Models to See and Critique like Photographers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18582v1)
- **Authors**: Daiqing Qi, Handong Zhao, Jing Shi, Simon Jenni, Yifei Fan, Franck Dernoncourt, Scott Cohen, Sheng Li
- **Abstract**: While editing directly from life, photographers have found it too difficult to see simultaneously both the blue and the sky. Photographer and curator, Szarkowski insightfully revealed one of the notable gaps between general and aesthetic visual understanding: while the former focuses on identifying the factual element in an image (sky), the latter transcends such object identification, viewing it instead as an aesthetic component--a pure color block (blue). Such fundamental distinctions between general (detection, localization, etc.) and aesthetic (color, lighting, composition, etc.) visual understanding present a significant challenge for Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). Although some recent works have made initial explorations, they are often limited to general and basic aesthetic commonsense. As a result, they frequently fall short in real-world scenarios (Fig. 1), which require extensive expertise--including photographic techniques, photo pre/post-processing knowledge, and more, to provide a detailed analysis and description. To fundamentally enhance the aesthetics understanding of MLLMs, we first introduce a novel dataset, PhotoCritique, derived from extensive discussions among professional photographers and enthusiasts, and characterized by the large scale, expertise, and diversity. Then, to better learn visual aesthetics from PhotoCritique, we furthur propose a novel model, PhotoEye, featuring a languageguided multi-view vision fusion mechanism to understand image aesthetics from multiple perspectives. Finally, we present a novel benchmark, PhotoBench, a comprehensive and professional benchmark for aesthetic visual understanding. On existing benchmarks and PhotoBench, our model demonstrates clear advantages over existing models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "The Photographer's Eye: Teaching Multimodal Large Language Models to See and Critique like Photographers":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of enabling Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to understand and critique images with the sophistication of a professional photographer. The authors identify limitations in existing MLLMs and datasets related to aesthetic understanding and low-level visual feature analysis. To overcome these, they introduce three key contributions:

1.  **PhotoCritique:** A large-scale dataset of over 450K images with 2.63M detailed aesthetic descriptions derived from online discussions among photographers.
2.  **PhotoEye:** A novel MLLM architecture featuring a language-guided multi-view vision fusion mechanism to better understand image aesthetics from multiple perspectives by fusing multiple pre-trained vision encoders with a language-guided fusion.
3.  **PhotoBench:** A professional benchmark for evaluating aesthetic visual understanding, comprising questions extracted from in-depth photography discussions.

The authors demonstrate that PhotoEye, trained on PhotoCritique and evaluated on PhotoBench, significantly outperforms existing models in understanding and critiquing image aesthetics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates significant novelty in its components.
    *   The PhotoCritique dataset is arguably the most novel aspect. Creating a large-scale dataset derived from authentic discussions among professional photographers is a significant effort and offers a unique resource for training MLLMs. The diversity of opinions and expertise captured in these discussions surpasses what could be achieved through traditional annotation methods.
    *   The PhotoEye architecture exhibits moderate novelty. The use of a multi-view vision fusion approach is not entirely new, but the language-guided fusion mechanism and the specific combination of vision encoders tailored for aesthetic understanding adds a valuable improvement.
    *   PhotoBench, the benchmark, is a valuable contribution to the field, providing a way to measure aesthetic understanding.

*   **Significance:** The paper holds notable significance:
    *   It addresses a critical gap in MLLM research: the lack of sophisticated aesthetic understanding. The ability to analyze and critique images with nuance is essential for applications in image recommendation, editing, and generation.
    *   The PhotoCritique dataset has the potential to become a widely used resource in the field, enabling further research and development in this area.
    *   The performance gains demonstrated by PhotoEye on PhotoBench highlight the importance of tailored architectures and training data for specialized visual understanding tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The dataset is large-scale and derived from expert sources, ensuring quality and diversity.
    *   The PhotoEye architecture is well-motivated and designed to address specific limitations of existing MLLMs.
    *   The PhotoBench benchmark provides a rigorous evaluation framework for aesthetic understanding.
    *   The paper presents thorough experimental results and ablations to support its claims.
    *   The writing is clear and well-organized.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the language-guided fusion mechanism is interesting, the paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the specific benefits of each vision encoder and how they contribute to the overall performance.
    *   The computational cost of using multiple vision encoders is a concern, and the paper could discuss the trade-offs between performance and efficiency.
    *   Although PhotoCritique is derived from a large community, the specific demographics of the source (Reddit’s PhotoCritique) could introduce bias that is not fully addressed.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by:
    *   Providing a new dataset and benchmark for aesthetic visual understanding.
    *   Inspiring the development of more specialized MLLM architectures for specific visual tasks.
    *   Encouraging the use of expert-derived data sources for training MLLMs.

**Score:** 8

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates significant novelty and significance by tackling the complex issue of aesthetic visual understanding in MLLMs. The creation of the PhotoCritique dataset, sourced from expert photographers, represents a major contribution to the field, filling a crucial gap in data resources. The PhotoEye architecture introduces a novel language-guided multi-view fusion mechanism, which effectively harnesses the strengths of multiple pre-trained vision encoders. While the computational cost of this approach could be a limitation, the demonstrated performance gains are compelling. The PhotoBench benchmark provides a means for rigorous evaluation of aesthetic understanding in MLLMs, enabling future research and development. Although the paper could benefit from a deeper analysis of the vision encoders and a more thorough discussion of potential biases in the data, the overall contribution is substantial. Therefore, a score of 8 reflects the paper's significant impact on the field and its potential to drive future research in aesthetic visual understanding for MLLMs.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### DS-Diffusion: Data Style-Guided Diffusion Model for Time-Series Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18584v1)
- **Authors**: Mingchun Sun, Rongqiang Zhao, Jie Liu
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models are the mainstream approach for time series generation tasks. However, existing diffusion models for time series generation require retraining the entire framework to introduce specific conditional guidance. There also exists a certain degree of distributional bias between the generated data and the real data, which leads to potential model biases in downstream tasks. Additionally, the complexity of diffusion models and the latent spaces leads to an uninterpretable inference process. To address these issues, we propose the data style-guided diffusion model (DS-Diffusion). In the DS-Diffusion, a diffusion framework based on style-guided kernels is developed to avoid retraining for specific conditions. The time-information based hierarchical denoising mechanism (THD) is developed to reduce the distributional bias between the generated data and the real data. Furthermore, the generated samples can clearly indicate the data style from which they originate. We conduct comprehensive evaluations using multiple public datasets to validate our approach. Experimental results show that, compared to the state-of-the-art model such as ImagenTime, the predictive score and the discriminative score decrease by 5.56% and 61.55%, respectively. The distributional bias between the generated data and the real data is further reduced, the inference process is also more interpretable. Moreover, by eliminating the need to retrain the diffusion model, the flexibility and adaptability of the model to specific conditions are also enhanced.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DS-Diffusion, a data style-guided diffusion model for time series generation.  It addresses limitations of existing diffusion models, such as the need to retrain for specific conditional guidance, distributional biases between generated and real data, and lack of interpretability. DS-Diffusion employs style-guided kernels within a diffusion framework, avoiding retraining for different conditions. It also uses a time-information based hierarchical denoising mechanism (THD) to reduce distributional bias and enhance interpretability by making the origin of generated samples clearer through extracted data styles. The approach is evaluated on multiple public datasets, demonstrating improved predictive and discriminative scores compared to state-of-the-art models, along with a reduction in distributional bias.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the combination of style-guided kernels and the time-information based hierarchical denoising mechanism (THD) within a diffusion model framework. Specifically, the approach aims to improve the flexibility, interpretability and distributional alignment of generated time series data compared to existing methods. The use of a style-guided kernels is indeed novel.
*   **Significance:** The paper aims to tackle significant challenges in time series generation: conditional control without retraining, reducing distributional bias, and enhancing interpretability. Improvements in these areas are crucial for the practical application of generative models in various domains. The claim to reduce retraining is also important and, if shown to be truly advantageous, would be helpful to the machine learning community.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies and addresses limitations of existing diffusion models for time series generation.
    *   The proposed DS-Diffusion architecture is well-motivated and technically sound. The style-guided kernels and THD appear to be effective components.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate quantitative improvements in predictive and discriminative scores compared to ImagenTime, and show a reduction in distributional bias (KL divergence, JS divergence, Wasserstein distance and KS Statistic).
    *   The qualitative comparisons (PCA, t-SNE, and visualization of probability density functions) provide further evidence for the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   The ablation study helps to understand the contribution of each component (STL, Transformer, Ttr and Tseas) to the overall performance.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the quantitative results indicate improvements, the percentage reductions in predictive score are relatively modest (5.56%). The significant improvement in discrimative score could potentially be explained by overfitting.
    *   The paper's focus on *style* may not be universally applicable or relevant to all time series generation tasks.  The definition of 'style' as trend and seasonality is somewhat narrow.
    *   While the paper presents qualitative visualization using PCA and t-SNE, they are a post-hoc evaluation and don't show how the intermediate frequency information is being used by the data driven approach.
    * The approach has a fair number of moving parts and an in-depth analysis of how these various componenets interact would be an advantage.

*   **Impact:** If the claims hold true, the DS-Diffusion model could have a moderate impact on time series generation, particularly in scenarios where conditional control, interpretability, and distributional alignment are crucial. The reduction of retraining would also improve the flexibility of these systems. However, future work with applications on specific domains are required.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a technically sound approach with empirical evidence demonstrating improvements over a state-of-the-art baseline. However, the improvements in the predictive and discriminative scores are relatively modest. Additionally, the novelty, although present, is somewhat incremental, consisting primarily of a specific combination of existing techniques tailored to time series. Overall, the contribution is above average, but not groundbreaking. The experiments appear to have been run correctly and appropriately.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Growing with Your Embodied Agent: A Human-in-the-Loop Lifelong Code Generation Framework for Long-Horizon Manipulation Skills
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18597v1)
- **Authors**: Yuan Meng, Zhenguo Sun, Max Fest, Xukun Li, Zhenshan Bing, Alois Knoll
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs)-based code generation for robotic manipulation has recently shown promise by directly translating human instructions into executable code, but existing methods remain noisy, constrained by fixed primitives and limited context windows, and struggle with long-horizon tasks. While closed-loop feedback has been explored, corrected knowledge is often stored in improper formats, restricting generalization and causing catastrophic forgetting, which highlights the need for learning reusable skills. Moreover, approaches that rely solely on LLM guidance frequently fail in extremely long-horizon scenarios due to LLMs' limited reasoning capability in the robotic domain, where such issues are often straightforward for humans to identify. To address these challenges, we propose a human-in-the-loop framework that encodes corrections into reusable skills, supported by external memory and Retrieval-Augmented Generation with a hint mechanism for dynamic reuse. Experiments on Ravens, Franka Kitchen, and MetaWorld, as well as real-world settings, show that our framework achieves a 0.93 success rate (up to 27% higher than baselines) and a 42% efficiency improvement in correction rounds. It can robustly solve extremely long-horizon tasks such as "build a house", which requires planning over 20 primitives.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LYRA, a human-in-the-loop lifelong learning framework for robotic manipulation. LYRA addresses limitations in LLM-based code generation for robotics, such as noise, limited context windows, and difficulty with long-horizon tasks.  The framework encodes user corrections into reusable skills, stores them in external memory, and uses retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) with hints for dynamic reuse. Experiments on several benchmarks and real-world settings demonstrate improved success rates and efficiency in correction rounds, enabling the solution of extremely long-horizon tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a reasonable degree of novelty. While human-in-the-loop learning and skill learning are not entirely new concepts in robotics, the specific combination of encoding corrections as reusable skills, leveraging external memory with RAG and hint mechanisms in the *code generation* context for *long-horizon manipulation* distinguishes this work.  The explicit focus on lifelong learning in robotic code generation with user corrections is a meaningful step forward. The idea of actively involving humans to rectify LLM errors in robot control is an active field, the integration skill learning to enhance code generation in this context is quite interesting. However, the core components are relatively well-known, thus the level of novelty isn't groundbreaking.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its practical impact on enabling robots to perform complex, long-horizon tasks reliably. The reported success rate improvements and efficiency gains compared to baselines suggest that the framework can significantly enhance the applicability of LLM-based code generation in real-world robotic scenarios.  The ability to handle tasks like "build a house" is a strong demonstration. The focus on real-world experiments further enhances the paper's significance. The paper contributes to addressing the major challenge of long-horizon robotic manipulation through efficient and reliable learning.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** LYRA offers a well-structured framework integrating human feedback, skill learning, external memory, and RAG.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides compelling empirical evidence of improved performance across diverse simulated and real-world environments.
    *   **Addresses Key Limitations:** The framework effectively addresses crucial limitations of existing LLM-based approaches in robotics, such as noise, limited context, and long-horizon planning.
    *   **Real-world Validation:** The real-world experiments showcase the framework's practical applicability.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Component Integration:** Although the framework itself is innovative, the individual components (skill learning, external memory, RAG) are individually well-established. The significance hinges primarily on the combination and application within this specific domain. A deeper exploration of the interplay between the human element and the framework's automated processes would be helpful.
    *   **LLM Reliance:** The framework still relies heavily on the LLM, and the quality of the generated code and skill learning depends on the LLM's capabilities. Further research into methods that can mitigate this reliance would be valuable. It would be helpful to delve more into how human guidance reduces LLM's mistakes.
    *   **Skill Generalization:** While the paper claims good generalization, there are no quantitative benchmarks to assess the generalization capabilities of the learned skill. Also, it is not clear how the skill library will scale in case of a very large variety of tasks.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research on LLM-based robotic manipulation, human-robot interaction, and lifelong learning. Other researchers could build upon LYRA's framework to develop more robust and adaptive robotic systems. The open-sourcing of the code will further enhance its impact.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for the Score:** The paper makes a solid contribution by addressing a relevant problem and providing encouraging empirical results. The integration of existing methods into a coherent robotic framework is a valuable contribution that bridges human instructions and robot execution. However, it doesn't introduce fundamental theoretical insights. The weaknesses identified above contribute to the moderately lower score.

### Training-Free Multi-Style Fusion Through Reference-Based Adaptive Modulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18602v1)
- **Authors**: Xu Liu, Yibo Lu, Xinxian Wang, Xinyu Wu
- **Abstract**: We propose Adaptive Multi-Style Fusion (AMSF), a reference-based training-free framework that enables controllable fusion of multiple reference styles in diffusion models. Most of the existing reference-based methods are limited by (a) acceptance of only one style image, thus prohibiting hybrid aesthetics and scalability to more styles, and (b) lack of a principled mechanism to balance several stylistic influences. AMSF mitigates these challenges by encoding all style images and textual hints with a semantic token decomposition module that is adaptively injected into every cross-attention layer of an frozen diffusion model. A similarity-aware re-weighting module then recalibrates, at each denoising step, the attention allocated to every style component, yielding balanced and user-controllable blends without any fine-tuning or external adapters. Both qualitative and quantitative evaluations show that AMSF produces multi-style fusion results that consistently outperform the state-of-the-art approaches, while its fusion design scales seamlessly to two or more styles. These capabilities position AMSF as a practical step toward expressive multi-style generation in diffusion models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Adaptive Multi-Style Fusion (AMSF), a training-free framework that enables controllable fusion of multiple reference styles in diffusion models for text-to-image generation. AMSF addresses limitations of existing reference-based methods by encoding all style images and textual hints with a semantic token decomposition module, injecting this information adaptively into the cross-attention layers of a frozen diffusion model. A similarity-aware re-weighting module then recalibrates attention allocated to each style component at each denoising step, allowing for balanced and user-controllable blends without fine-tuning or external adapters. The paper presents qualitative and quantitative evaluations demonstrating AMSF's superiority over state-of-the-art approaches in producing multi-style fusion results, scaling seamlessly to multiple styles.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's primary novelty lies in the architecture of AMSF, specifically the combination of semantic token decomposition and similarity-aware attention re-weighting. The semantic token decomposition helps to isolate the subject prompt, preventing it from overwhelming style tokens when multiple style prompts are provided. The Similarity-Aware Attention Re-weighting mechanism enables adaptive control of style strength without requiring manual tuning, which is a significant improvement over previous methods. It is *incremental* novelty since individual components draw inspiration from previous works (e.g., attention re-weighting in other contexts, SOC framing of diffusion). However, combining these in this specific way for multi-style diffusion is novel.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of the paper stems from its practical applicability and potential to democratize creative control in diffusion models. By offering a training-free, multi-style fusion approach, AMSF allows users to explore hybrid aesthetics more easily and control the influence of different styles in the generated output. This could have a notable impact on areas like content creation, design, and art. The seamless scaling to multiple styles further enhances its practicality. However, the limited dataset size and specific focus on relatively simple style combinations limit the generalizability of the results.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-free approach:** Eliminates the need for fine-tuning, making it easily adaptable.
    *   **Multi-style fusion:** Supports more than one style reference, enabling hybrid aesthetics.
    *   **Adaptive control:** The similarity-aware re-weighting module provides user-friendly style control.
    *   **Strong results:** Outperforms state-of-the-art approaches in qualitative and quantitative evaluations.
    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly identifies the shortcomings of existing methods and proposes a solution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited dataset**: Experiments are performed on a relatively small and stylized dataset.
    *   **Limited scope of style combinations**: Style pairs are chosen to be semantically meaningful, which may not reflect real-world artistic intentions.
    *   **Lack of exploration of high-resolution results**:  The paper does not delve into specific optimizations or challenges encountered when generating high-resolution images.
    *   **Reliance on pre-trained models:** The performance relies on the quality of the underlying pre-trained diffusion model and CLIP embeddings.

*   **Potential Impact:** AMSF could serve as a valuable tool for artists and designers seeking greater control over style in text-to-image generation. The method's user-friendly approach and ability to handle multiple styles could inspire the development of more expressive and customizable diffusion models. The focus on architectural innovation over fine-tuning contributes a general principle for diffusion-based style transfer.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a technically sound and practically relevant contribution to the field of text-to-image generation. While the novelty is incremental, the combined elements of semantic token decomposition and similarity-aware re-weighting deliver a significant improvement in multi-style fusion capabilities. The training-free nature of AMSF further enhances its accessibility. Although limited by scope of dataset and lack of evaluation in complicated settings, its potential impact on creative workflows warrants a good score.

### SynSonic: Augmenting Sound Event Detection through Text-to-Audio Diffusion ControlNet and Effective Sample Filtering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18603v1)
- **Authors**: Jiarui Hai, Mounya Elhilali
- **Abstract**: Data synthesis and augmentation are essential for Sound Event Detection (SED) due to the scarcity of temporally labeled data. While augmentation methods like SpecAugment and Mix-up can enhance model performance, they remain constrained by the diversity of existing samples. Recent generative models offer new opportunities, yet their direct application to SED is challenging due to the lack of precise temporal annotations and the risk of introducing noise through unreliable filtering. To address these challenges and enable generative-based augmentation for SED, we propose SynSonic, a data augmentation method tailored for this task. SynSonic leverages text-to-audio diffusion models guided by an energy-envelope ControlNet to generate temporally coherent sound events. A joint score filtering strategy with dual classifiers ensures sample quality, and we explore its practical integration into training pipelines. Experimental results show that SynSonic improves Polyphonic Sound Detection Scores (PSDS1 and PSDS2), enhancing both temporal localization and sound class discrimination.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the SynSonic paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SynSonic, a data augmentation method designed for sound event detection (SED).  It addresses the challenge of limited labeled data for SED tasks by leveraging text-to-audio (T2A) diffusion models. SynSonic uses an energy-envelope ControlNet to generate temporally coherent sound events and employs a dual-classifier joint ranking strategy for robust sample selection, mitigating noise and bias. The selected synthetic samples are then used to augment the training dataset, improving both temporal localization and sound class discrimination in SED models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper combines several existing technologies (T2A models, ControlNet, sample selection, and SED) in a novel way specifically tailored to the needs of SED.  The key novelty lies in the integration of these components into a coherent framework that addresses the specific challenges of augmenting strongly labeled data for SED.  Using ControlNet for temporal control of sound events within the T2A generation process is a unique contribution. The dual classifier filtering strategy is also a novel contribution, addressing a specific issue of bias in a single score models, a common challenge encountered when using generative models for augmentation.

*   **Significance:** Data augmentation is a crucial tool in machine learning. Applying it to SED opens up opportunities to build more robust and generalizable systems. By demonstrating improvements on a standard dataset (DESED), the paper shows practical value and potential impact. SynSonic addresses a significant bottleneck in SED by making it easier to create labeled training data. The experiments are fairly comprehensive, exploring several parameter settings. The comparison to existing methods in Table 1 is helpful and shows the advantage of using the Energy ControlNet over alternative generation approaches.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The combination of ControlNet for precise temporal structure and T2A models for diversity is a strong point.
    *   The dual-classifier sample filtering strategy offers a more robust selection process.
    *   The experiments are comprehensive and well-documented.
    *   The paper provides clear insights into practical considerations for using synthetic data in SED training.
    *   The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While novel in its combination, the individual components of SynSonic (T2A, ControlNet, etc.) are not novel.  The innovation comes from their integration.
    *   The dependency on an external T2A model and a pre-trained AudioSet classifier can limit portability and require significant computational resources. Fine-tuning these modules might further improve the results but adds complexity.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the types of errors that SynSonic is able to correct in the SED models. Error analyses are valuable to help reveal the weaknesses and strengths of proposed approach.
    *   The experiments do not directly compare against other *existing* data augmentation methods (such as SpecAugment with a better tuning, or MixUp).

*   **Potential Influence:** SynSonic has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical and effective method for generating synthetic data for SED. It could encourage further research into generative model-based data augmentation for SED and other tasks that require precisely labeled temporal data. The approach is likely generalizable to other audio-related tasks.

*   **Justification for Score:**
The paper makes a solid, well-executed contribution by combining several technologies to create a practical data augmentation method for SED. While the individual components are not entirely new, the *combination* and the targeted design specifically for SED are novel and significant. The results demonstrate clear improvements on a benchmark dataset. The code availability enhances reproducibility and adoption by other researchers. The experiments show solid improvements on metrics such as PSDS1 and PSDS2. Thus, while the fundamental building blocks exist, the work makes a sufficient and practically-validated contribution to warrant a score of 7.5.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Score**: 7/10

### FlexSED: Towards Open-Vocabulary Sound Event Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18606v1)
- **Authors**: Jiarui Hai, Helin Wang, Weizhe Guo, Mounya Elhilali
- **Abstract**: Despite recent progress in large-scale sound event detection (SED) systems capable of handling hundreds of sound classes, existing multi-class classification frameworks remain fundamentally limited. They cannot process free-text sound queries, which enable more flexible and user-friendly interaction, and they lack zero-shot capabilities and offer poor few-shot adaptability. Although text-query-based separation methods have been explored, they primarily focus on source separation and are ill-suited for SED tasks that require precise temporal localization and efficient detection across large and diverse sound vocabularies. In this paper, we propose FlexSED, an open-vocabulary sound event detection system. FlexSED builds on a pretrained audio SSL model and the CLAP text encoder, introducing an encoder-decoder composition and an adaptive fusion strategy to enable effective continuous training from pretrained weights. To ensure robust supervision, it also employs large language models (LLMs) to assist in event query selection during training, addressing challenges related to missing labels. As a result, FlexSED achieves superior performance compared to vanilla SED models on AudioSet-Strong, while demonstrating strong zero-shot and few-shot capabilities. We release the code and pretrained models to support future research and applications based on FlexSED.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the FlexSED paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FlexSED, an open-vocabulary sound event detection (OV-SED) system designed to overcome limitations of traditional multi-class classification and separation-based approaches. FlexSED leverages a pre-trained audio SSL model and the CLAP text encoder, using an encoder-decoder architecture and adaptive fusion strategy to integrate audio and text information.  It also employs a large language model (LLM)-assisted negative query filtering method to address issues related to missing labels in the training data.  The system demonstrates improved performance compared to vanilla SED models on AudioSet-Strong and exhibits promising zero-shot and few-shot capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** While individual components of FlexSED (pre-trained models, encoder-decoder architecture, adaptive fusion) have been explored in other contexts, the *specific combination and application to OV-SED* is where the primary novelty lies. The use of an LLM to intelligently filter negative samples is a noteworthy contribution, addressing a critical practical challenge in this domain. The novelty isn't groundbreaking individual method, but rather in carefully combining existing techniques and addressing the label scarcity challenge in open vocabulary SED.

**Significance:** The move towards open-vocabulary sound event detection is significant because it addresses a major limitation of current SED systems: their reliance on predefined class sets. FlexSED represents a step in this direction. Demonstrating improved zero-shot and few-shot performance expands the practical applicability of SED. Furthermore, the code and model release facilitates further research and application of the proposed technology. The integration with other tasks such as text-to-audio evaluation and audio data annotation highlights its broader impact.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Performance:** The paper demonstrates substantial performance gains compared to baseline SED models, validating the effectiveness of the proposed architecture and training strategy.
*   **OV-SED Capability:** The focus on open-vocabulary detection addresses a crucial limitation in existing SED systems.
*   **LLM-Assisted Negative Sampling:** The clever use of LLMs to filter potentially conflicting negative samples is a key strength, making the approach more robust to noisy or incomplete labels.
*   **Zero-shot and Few-shot Learning:** The ability to perform well on unseen classes (zero-shot) and adapt quickly to new classes with minimal data (few-shot) enhances the practical utility of the model.
*   **Reproducibility:** The release of code and pre-trained models encourages further research and adoption by the community.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Advances:** The improvements come from combining existing methods, rather than introducing a radically new technique. While the combination is effective, it might be viewed as evolutionary rather than revolutionary.
*   **Dependency on External Models:** The approach depends on the performance and availability of the CLAP and audio SSL backbone models. Future improvements in these base models directly translate to FlexSED. The heavy reliance on pretrained components might limit opportunities for fundamentally new architectural innovations.
*   **Computational Cost:** While the encoder-decoder architecture improves efficiency, the computational cost of LLM-based negative filtering is not explicitly addressed and might be a bottleneck. The paper mentions 128GB memory requirement, hinting at resource constraints. A more detailed analysis of computational complexity would strengthen the paper.
*   **Limited Zero-Shot improvement in all metrics**: Zero shot improvement is high only in the PSDS1T metric but PSDS1A isn't nearly as high, indicating the overall performance isn't as good as claimed.

**Justification for the Score:**

FlexSED is a valuable contribution to the field of sound event detection, specifically because it tackles the critical limitation of closed-vocabulary systems. The innovative use of LLMs to improve training robustness is well-motivated and empirically validated.  The demonstrated improvements in zero-shot and few-shot learning expand the applicability of SED.

However, the approach builds upon existing techniques and depends on external models.  The individual components are not radically novel, and a more in-depth analysis of computational cost and the zero shot limitations (in PSDS1A) would be beneficial. Taking this into account, a score in the upper-middle range seems appropriate.

### Reflect before Act: Proactive Error Correction in Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18607v1)
- **Authors**: Qiuhai Zeng, Sarvesh Rajkumar, Di Wang, Narendra Gyanchandani, Wenbo Yan
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in interactive decision-making tasks, but existing methods often struggle with error accumulation and lack robust self-correction mechanisms. We introduce "Reflect before Act" (REBACT), a novel approach that enhances LLM-based decision-making by introducing a critical reflect step prior to taking the next action. This approach allows for immediate error correction, ensuring smooth action path and adaptibity to environment feedback. We evaluate REBACT on three diverse interactive environments: ALFWorld, WebShop, and TextCraft. Our results demonstrate that REBACT significantly outperforms strong baselines, improving success rates by up to 24% on WebShop (achieving 61%), 6.72% on ALFWorld (achieving 98.51%), and 0.5% on TextCraft (achieving 99.5%) using Claude3.5-sonnet as the underlying LLM. Further analysis reveals that REBACT's performance improvements are achieved with only a few modification steps, demonstrating its computational efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Reflect before Act: Proactive Error Correction in Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Reflect before Act" (REBACT), a novel approach to improve the interactive decision-making capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs).  REBACT integrates a reflection step *before* each action, where the LLM evaluates previous actions and corrects errors before proceeding.  This iterative self-reflection aims to ensure a smooth and accurate action path, adapting to environmental feedback effectively.  The authors evaluate REBACT on three interactive environments: ALFWorld, WebShop, and TextCraft.  The results demonstrate significant improvements in success rates compared to strong baselines like ReAct and ADAPT, while maintaining computational efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty of the paper lies in its proactive error correction mechanism within interactive decision-making. Instead of reactive error correction after a task fails (like Reflexion) or relying on subtask decomposition (like ADAPT), REBACT explicitly prompts the LLM to reflect on the *previous* actions *before* taking the next action. This "reflect before act" paradigm is a valuable contribution. Other work is often focused on the 'act-only' and/or 'act-reason' approaches. This stands as a distinct shift towards self-correction.

*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from the observed performance improvements in interactive environments. Achieving higher success rates (especially notable on WebShop) is important, as interactive tasks with feedback mimic many real-world applications. The efficiency achieved is also significant. Moreover, the comparison with existing models (ReAct, ADAPT) helps to clarify the contribution of the authors.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper identifies a critical limitation of existing LLM agents in interactive environments: error accumulation.
    *   **Well-defined approach:** REBACT is presented as a clear and easily understandable framework.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The evaluation across three diverse datasets (ALFWorld, WebShop, TextCraft) demonstrates the robustness of REBACT.
    *   **Strong results:** REBACT consistently outperforms strong baselines, showcasing the effectiveness of proactive error correction.
    *   **Efficiency Analysis:** Demonstrating that REBACT does not necessarily require more LLM calls than other approaches is a significant finding. This addresses a common concern about reflective methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited model exploration:** The experiments focus solely on the Claude3.5-sonnet model. It's necessary to understand if REBACT generalizes well to other LLMs.
    *   **Prompt dependence:** As with any LLM-based approach, the performance of REBACT is dependent on the quality of the prompt used. The paper does not include extensive experimentation on prompt variations or robustness to prompt changes.
    *   **Modifiable action restriction:** The paper acknowledges a limitation: not all actions can be easily modified or corrected in real-world scenarios. This requires careful identification of actions suitable for modification and formulation of corresponding corrective strategies.
    *   **Feedback dependence:** REBACT's effectiveness relies on receiving sufficient environmental feedback to enable self-reflection. Tasks with sparse or uninformative feedback may limit its applicability.
    *   **Lack of theoretical analysis:** There is not a lot of theoretical analysis behind why ReBACT is working so well.
    *   **Not well explained what happens when ReBACT makes a mistake**. If ReBACT corrects an error, and it ends up being incorrect, then what happens? The paper doesn't seem to address this question.

*   **Potential Influence:** This approach has the potential to influence the development of more robust and reliable LLM agents for interactive tasks.  The idea of proactive error correction is a useful principle for designing future methods. The findings may also encourage further research into the integration of reflection and planning within a single LLM call for efficiency.

**Justification for Score:**

While REBACT presents a valuable and well-executed approach to error correction in LLM agents, there are several issues that need to be further addressed. While the contribution is distinct, it lacks depth in some areas. This paper can encourage future development of more robust, reflective, and reliable LLM agents in interactive environments.

Score: 7

- **Score**: 7/10

### Flow marching for a generative PDE foundation model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18611v1)
- **Authors**: Zituo Chen, Sili Deng
- **Abstract**: Pretraining on large-scale collections of PDE-governed spatiotemporal trajectories has recently shown promise for building generalizable models of dynamical systems. Yet most existing PDE foundation models rely on deterministic Transformer architectures, which lack generative flexibility for many science and engineering applications. We propose Flow Marching, an algorithm that bridges neural operator learning with flow matching motivated by an analysis of error accumulation in physical dynamical systems, and we build a generative PDE foundation model on top of it. By jointly sampling the noise level and the physical time step between adjacent states, the model learns a unified velocity field that transports a noisy current state toward its clean successor, reducing long-term rollout drift while enabling uncertainty-aware ensemble generations. Alongside this core algorithm, we introduce a Physics-Pretrained Variational Autoencoder (P2VAE) to embed physical states into a compact latent space, and an efficient Flow Marching Transformer (FMT) that combines a diffusion-forcing scheme with latent temporal pyramids, achieving up to 15x greater computational efficiency than full-length video diffusion models and thereby enabling large-scale pretraining at substantially reduced cost. We curate a corpus of ~2.5M trajectories across 12 distinct PDE families and train suites of P2VAEs and FMTs at multiple scales. On downstream evaluation, we benchmark on unseen Kolmogorov turbulence with few-shot adaptation, demonstrate long-term rollout stability over deterministic counterparts, and present uncertainty-stratified ensemble results, highlighting the importance of generative PDE foundation models for real-world applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Flow Marching, a novel algorithm for building generative PDE foundation models. Flow Marching bridges neural operator learning with flow matching, aiming to improve long-term rollout stability and enable uncertainty-aware ensemble generation. The algorithm learns a unified velocity field that transports noisy states towards their clean successors. The authors also introduce a Physics-Pretrained Variational Autoencoder (P2VAE) for efficient state embedding and a Flow Marching Transformer (FMT) architecture that combines diffusion forcing with latent temporal pyramids to enhance computational efficiency. They curate a large corpus of PDE trajectories (~2.5M) and train their models, demonstrating improved performance on downstream tasks like few-shot adaptation to turbulence, long-term rollout stability, and uncertainty quantification.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the bridging of deterministic neural operators with stochastic flow matching through the Flow Marching algorithm and the bridge parameter *k*. This provides a theoretically grounded approach to inject noise for uncertainty awareness, while retaining the deterministic accuracy of neural operators. The architecture P2VAE-FMT, and data-curation, while impressive engineering, is less conceptually novel and more of an effective implementation choice given the problem constraints.
*   **Significance:** PDE foundation models are a burgeoning field, and generative capabilities are crucial for uncertainty quantification and data assimilation. The claimed ability to control IC uncertainty and improve long-term rollout in a principled manner is highly significant, if thoroughly validated. The experiments, while showing promise, are not fully exhaustive. The use of publicly available datasets, though commendable for reproducibility, limits the demonstration of the model's ability to generalize to entirely new, complex physical systems. Demonstrating the utility of the generative model (i.e., via ensemble generation for data assimilation or robust control) would drastically improve the impact.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The Flow Marching algorithm is theoretically well-motivated, with a clear connection to error accumulation in dynamical systems.
    *   The P2VAE-FMT architecture is designed for efficiency, allowing for large-scale pretraining.
    *   The curation of a large, diverse PDE dataset is a valuable contribution.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate promising performance on a range of tasks, including few-shot adaptation and long-term rollout stability.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the theoretical motivation is strong, the paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the algorithm's behavior in different regimes.  How does the interplay between k and timestep affect performance?
    *   The experiments lack in-depth ablation studies to rigorously assess the impact of each component of the model (e.g., the diffusion forcing scheme, the temporal pyramids).
    *   The paper doesn't strongly validate the downstream utility of the *generative* capabilities, focusing mainly on improvements in deterministic prediction.
    *   The description of the experimental setup is somewhat dense; some clarification would improve reproducibility.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of PDE foundation models by introducing a theoretically grounded approach to generative modeling that could lead to more robust and uncertainty-aware predictions. The P2VAE-FMT architecture could serve as a blueprint for efficient generative PDE models. Further work demonstrating the practical applications of this uncertainty quantification (e.g., in data assimilation or robust control) would greatly amplify the impact.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and theoretically well-motivated algorithm (Flow Marching) for building generative PDE foundation models. The efficient architecture and the large-scale dataset are valuable contributions. However, the paper falls short of a higher score due to the limited demonstration of the downstream utility of the generative model for uncertainty quantification and the lack of extensive ablation studies. While the experimental results are promising, a more rigorous validation of the model's components and a clearer emphasis on the benefits of ensemble generation for practical applications are needed.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Prompt-Guided Dual Latent Steering for Inversion Problems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18619v1)
- **Authors**: Yichen Wu, Xu Liu, Chenxuan Zhao, Xinyu Wu
- **Abstract**: Inverting corrupted images into the latent space of diffusion models is challenging. Current methods, which encode an image into a single latent vector, struggle to balance structural fidelity with semantic accuracy, leading to reconstructions with semantic drift, such as blurred details or incorrect attributes. To overcome this, we introduce Prompt-Guided Dual Latent Steering (PDLS), a novel, training-free framework built upon Rectified Flow models for their stable inversion paths. PDLS decomposes the inversion process into two complementary streams: a structural path to preserve source integrity and a semantic path guided by a prompt. We formulate this dual guidance as an optimal control problem and derive a closed-form solution via a Linear Quadratic Regulator (LQR). This controller dynamically steers the generative trajectory at each step, preventing semantic drift while ensuring the preservation of fine detail without costly, per-image optimization. Extensive experiments on FFHQ-1K and ImageNet-1K under various inversion tasks, including Gaussian deblurring, motion deblurring, super-resolution and freeform inpainting, demonstrate that PDLS produces reconstructions that are both more faithful to the original image and better aligned with the semantic information than single-latent baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Prompt-Guided Dual Latent Steering (PDLS), a novel, training-free framework for image inversion problems using Rectified Flow models.  PDLS decomposes the inversion process into two streams: a structural path guided by a null prompt to preserve source image integrity and a semantic path guided by a text prompt to incorporate semantic information.  The paper formulates this dual guidance as an optimal control problem solved with a Linear Quadratic Regulator (LQR).  This controller dynamically steers the generative trajectory, preventing semantic drift and preserving fine detail without per-image optimization.  Experiments on FFHQ-1K and ImageNet-1K demonstrate that PDLS produces reconstructions that are both more faithful to the original image and better aligned with semantic information than single-latent baselines across various inversion tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The main novelty lies in the dual-path approach to image inversion, combining structural and semantic information during the generative process.  The use of an LQR controller to dynamically steer the latent state is also a significant contribution.  Building upon Rectified Flow models is not entirely new, but the way it's integrated with the dual-path approach enhances the stability and predictability of the inversion.  The time-decaying steering schedule is a simple but effective technique for balancing early guidance with later detail preservation.

*   **Significance:** Image inversion is a crucial task for many downstream applications like image editing, restoration, and manipulation. The limitations of current single-latent methods in balancing fidelity and semantic accuracy are well-addressed by PDLS.  The training-free aspect is a major advantage, making it easily adaptable to different pre-trained diffusion models. The experimental results show consistent improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods in terms of both perceptual quality (LPIPS) and fidelity (PSNR, SSIM). The ablation studies further validate the effectiveness of the dual-path approach and the importance of prompt informativeness.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The dual-path inversion is a conceptually clear and effective way to address the trade-off between structural fidelity and semantic accuracy.
    *   The use of an LQR controller provides a principled way to dynamically balance the two paths.
    *   The training-free nature makes it practical and easily applicable to different diffusion models.
    *   The experiments are comprehensive, covering various inversion tasks and datasets.
    *   The ablation studies offer valuable insights into the design choices.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The performance of the semantic path is highly dependent on the prompt, as highlighted by the prompt informativeness study. While the paper provides guidelines for prompt design, it might still require some trial and error to achieve optimal results.
    *   While the method is training-free, it relies on pre-trained Rectified Flow models. The performance might be limited by the quality and domain of these pre-trained models.
    *   The implementation details section could be slightly more thorough, especially regarding the choice of hyperparameters.
    *   The improvements, while consistent, are not always dramatically larger than existing methods, suggesting the need for further improvements or extensions.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the field of image inversion by providing a more robust and versatile approach that balances fidelity and semantic control. The training-free nature and clear conceptual framework make it likely to be adopted and extended by other researchers. It opens up avenues for further research on task-specific rewards, multi-modal guidance, and video restoration.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a well-motivated, conceptually novel, and experimentally validated approach to image inversion.  While the building blocks (Rectified Flows, LQR control) are not entirely new, their integration into a dual-path framework for semantic-aware inversion is a significant contribution. The training-free nature and consistent improvements across various tasks make this a practical and impactful method.  The limitations, such as prompt sensitivity and reliance on pre-trained models, are acknowledged and don't detract significantly from the overall contribution.
